  `release-evidence/**` before uploading evidence: native production config
  before/after bump, GSAV preflight, app/Gradle version metadata, release
  artifact verifier JSON, and generated APK version metadata output. The production-config
  verifier now rejects removal of that uploaded evidence-output directory.
- Hardened Android/iOS route evidence checks so completed route rows now need
  build profile, OS version, WebView/WKWebView version, GSAV host identity,
  command or manual action context, and final embedded WebView URL for embedded
  routes. Weak device rows can no longer satisfy publish readiness by saying
  only that a route passed.
- Hardened negative-case evidence checks so completed `Android/iOS` negative
  rows now need build profile, Android OS/WebView version, iOS OS/WKWebView
  version, and trigger/action context in addition to both-platform evidence
  paths and the case-specific observed signal.
- Hardened generated-version and dry-run evidence checks. Generated-version
  evidence may use `aapt dump badging`, `apkanalyzer manifest print`,
  `bundletool dump manifest`, or `release-evidence/apk-version-metadata.txt`
  with `versionCode`; if generated APK metadata output is unavailable, the row
  now needs an owner/revisit exception plus an accepted generated APK metadata
  source. Release dry-run rows now must
  mention uploaded `release-evidence/**` command outputs, not only generic
  evidence upload status.
- Hardened IPv6 local/private production URL checks across release safety
  verifiers. Production config, release artifact, and production `.gsav`
  range-probe checks now reject bracketed IPv6 loopback, unique-local,
  link-local, and IPv4-mapped private hosts rather than only IPv4 literals.
- Hardened release artifact verification so `android:debuggable="true"` in the
  merged manifest fails `npm run verify:release-artifact`. Publish-readiness
  evidence rows for the APK artifact must now summarize both cleartext disabled
  and non-debuggable manifest status.
- Hardened artifact checksum evidence. `npm run verify:release-artifact` now
  emits both `apkSha256` and `manifestSha256`, and release-readiness rejects APK
  artifact evidence rows that only provide a generic checksum without separate
  APK and merged-manifest checksum details.
- Hardened release-readiness evidence URL validation so HTTPS artifact/release
  links on local or private hosts no longer count as durable external evidence.
  Local evidence must remain an existing `docs/qa-evidence/...` file, and
  external evidence must be a non-local HTTPS CI/artifact/release URL.
- Aligned `scripts/gsav-native-preflight.js` with the release evidence contract
  by changing the `.gsav` byte-range request to `Range: bytes=0-0`. The QA
  runbook now states that exact probe, and the implementation plan now includes
  a canonical release-candidate checklist plus explicit app-route,
  composition-exception, and evidence-source rules.
- Ran three read-only review agents for architecture elegance, release/V&V gate
  strength, and bridge/auth-init correctness. The architecture review found the
  current hybrid structure sound: `app/` is route glue, React Native owns the
  product shell, `features/player` owns embed lifecycle, and gsav-hosting owns
  runtime playback. The remaining elegance work is naming/isolation around the
  hosted `/explore` exception, follow-button presentation inside social, and a
  possible bridge-protocol facade.
- The release/V&V review found that `verify:release-readiness` correctly blocks
  missing rows today, but future completed rows need stronger anti-stale checks
  before a passing gate can be treated as publish approval. The implementation
  plan now tracks hardening for current candidate identity comparison, generated
  `versionCode` parsing, runtime-smoke evidence, duplicate row freshness,
  platform-specific negative evidence, and dry-run evidence summaries.
- Hardened `scripts/verify-release-readiness.js` so release signoff now checks
  28 release-readiness evidence rows, including JS runtime smoke and
  installed-release APK smoke. Completed rows
  are compared to current `git HEAD`, `app.json`/`package.json` version, and
  Android `versionCode` where relevant; generic external URLs are rejected in
  favor of trusted GitHub CI/artifact/release URLs under `opsiclear/diveo` or
  `opsiclear/gsav-hosting`; duplicate rows use the latest
  matching table entry; generated Gradle/app/APK `versionCode` values must match
  numerically; installed-release APK smoke must prove the exact APK was
  installed, launched, and exercised production `/explore`, diagnostics, and
  watch routes; combined negative rows need distinct Android and iOS evidence
  paths unless they record an owner/revisit exception; and dry-run rows must
  include production preflight, APK verifier, evidence upload status, artifact
  name, run URL, and checksum details.
- Ran a follow-up three-agent plan review for architecture, release validation,
  and documentation coherence. The plan now records the stale `App.tsx`/`index.ts`
  root-entry risk, remaining catalog/social/player composition refinement, the
  service/scene DTO narrowing target, historical status for
  `docs/gsav-hosting-port.md`, `GSAV_RANGE_PROBE_URL` versus preflight alias
  naming, and `GSAV_FIRST_FRAME` as observational when the renderer reports a
  ready/error state without first frame.
- Removed the stale classic Expo root files `App.tsx` and `index.ts`. The app
  entry remains `expo-router/entry`, with Sentry initialization owned by
  `features/app-shell/RootLayout.tsx`. The import-boundary verifier now reads
  root-level `App.tsx`/`index.ts` if they exist and fails the gate so a second
  app root cannot be reintroduced unnoticed.
- Narrowed the GSAV service and scene presentation boundary. `services/gsav.ts`
  now exports only the catalog client, normalization helpers, and catalog types
  diveo uses instead of
  `export *` from `@opsiclear/gsav-client`. `features/scene/sceneTypes.ts`
  now defines a minimal `SceneItem` DTO, and
  `features/social/loadSavedScenesByBackendIds.ts` maps saved catalog items into
  that DTO so scene UI does not depend on full catalog-client fields.
- Hardened command evidence output for host/runtime validation. Both
  `scripts/gsav-native-preflight.js` and `scripts/gsav-native-runtime-smoke.js`
  now emit `nodeVersion`, `npmVersion`, `diveoCommit`, and
  `gsavHostingCommit` when available, so future raw QA evidence contains the
  command metadata required by `docs/GSAV_NATIVE_QA.md`.
- Hardened release-readiness runtime-smoke checks so completed JS runtime rows
  must summarize Node/npm command metadata and GSAV host/build identity in
  addition to embed-mode, chrome suppression, and bridge readiness signals.
- Hardened the import-boundary allowlist so every sibling-feature exception now
  carries an enforced category, rationale, owner, and exit criteria. `npm run
  verify:import-boundaries` prints the documented exception set, and focused
  tests fail if a future exception is added without allowlist documentation.
- Extracted native WebView bridge-message routing into
  `features/player/nativeBridgeMessage.ts`. Focused tests now prove the native
  host ignores untrusted page origins, applies session on trusted
  `GSAV_AUTH_READY` without routing it as progress, accepts missing platform
  page URLs only under the navigation-gate trust model, and clears resume
  progress when a trusted `GSAV_ENDED` message reaches the native host path.
- Extracted native WebView navigation decisions into
  `features/player/nativeNavigation.ts`. Focused tests now prove same-origin
  GSAV routes stay inside the WebView, external `http(s)` URLs are blocked from
  the WebView and marked for system-browser handoff, and malformed/non-origin
  schemes fail closed.
- Extracted native WebView load/error/retry state into
  `features/player/nativeLoadState.ts`. Focused tests now prove load errors
  hide the loading overlay and show a stable retryable error, new loads clear
  stale errors, and retry clears stale errors while incrementing the WebView key
  for remount.
- Extracted native WebView bridge-error overlay state into
  `features/player/nativeBridgeOverlay.ts`. Focused tests now prove trusted
  unsupported `GSAV_CAPABILITIES`, `GSAV_ERROR`, and bridge-version mismatch
  messages surface native errors, while malformed/progress-only messages
  preserve current error state and ready/reset clears stale bridge errors.
- Used three focused review agents to reassess architecture elegance,
  verification/validation gates, and documentation flow. The follow-up plan
  update made the release-candidate checklist the authoritative runbook,
  clarified the payload-candidate version rule, moved copyable evidence row
  templates next to the QA evidence table, documented the limits of
  marker-based docs drift checks, and added installed-release APK smoke as an
  explicit release validation step.
- Hardened the release workflow secret gate so a manual
  `workflow_dispatch` run with `publish_release=true` fails when required
  production GSAV/Supabase/range-probe secrets are missing. Non-publishing dry
  runs can still skip when secrets are absent, but skipped runs do not count as
  release evidence. `verify:native-production-config` now rejects removal of
  that publish-intent failure branch and reports
  `failsPublishIntentWithoutSecrets` in its checked summary.
- Added `playwright-core` as a dev dependency for deterministic CI runtime-smoke
  execution without downloading browser binaries. `scripts/gsav-native-runtime-smoke.js`
  now accepts `GSAV_RUNTIME_SMOKE_CHROMIUM_EXECUTABLE`, and the release workflow
  runs `npm run gsav:runtime-smoke` against the production GSAV URL after
  preflight, saving `release-evidence/gsav-runtime-smoke.json`. Production
  config verification rejects workflows that omit the runtime-smoke step or its
  evidence file, and release-readiness dry-run evidence rows must now mention
  the runtime-smoke output.
- Added `scripts/verify-release-evidence-bundle.js`,
  `scripts/verify-release-evidence-bundle.test.mjs`, and
  `npm run verify:release-evidence-bundle`. The verifier validates generated
  `release-evidence/**` contents before upload: required files, checksum
  manifest, dry-run summary fields, APK/manifest hashes, production
  preflight/runtime-smoke JSON, release artifact verifier output, and generated
  `versionCode` consistency. The release workflow now runs it after writing
  `dry-run-summary.json` and before uploading the artifact, and
  `verify:native-production-config` rejects workflows that omit that step.
- Added `scripts/capture-android-installed-smoke.js`,
  `scripts/capture-android-installed-smoke.test.mjs`, and
  `npm run android:installed-smoke`. The helper installs the exact supplied APK
  with `adb`, opens `gsav://explore`, `gsav://gsav-diagnostics`, and
  `gsav://watch/test`, captures WebView/RN logs, and writes a durable
  manual-review evidence file for the release-installed APK smoke QA row.
- Ran a fresh three-agent structure/V&V/docs review of the current native/web
  split. The review found the React Native product shell plus
  `features/player` embedded GSAV runtime boundary sound, with remaining
  elegance risk concentrated in player-host exclusivity enforcement,
  catalog/social follow composition, and social GSAV-client access. The
  implementation plan now starts with a "How To Use This Plan" entry point,
  choose-your-path gate matrix, work-package state model, verification versus
  validation owner matrix, sibling allowlist budget, and explicit WP-1 closure
  tasks. The QA runbook now includes a negative fixture inventory so manual
  validation rows must name exact triggers instead of relying on vague
  "manual check passed" claims.
- Implemented player-host exclusivity in
  `scripts/verify-import-boundaries.js`. The import-boundary verifier now
  rejects non-test `react-native-webview` imports outside
  `features/player/GsavWebView.tsx`, non-test `<iframe>` player hosts outside
  `features/player/GsavWebView.web.tsx`, and native embed URL construction
  outside `features/player`. Focused tests cover the allowed player host files,
  non-player rejection cases, and the helper-level ownership rule. The verifier
  output now prints `playerHostOwnershipRules` alongside the sibling-feature and
  external-package policies.
- Resolved the catalog/social follow-composition edge by adding
  `features/social/FollowButton.tsx` as the social-owned follow action UI.
  `features/catalog/CreatorScreen.tsx` now passes creator identity, initial
  follower count, a follower-count display callback, and login navigation into
  that component instead of importing `useGsavFollow` directly. The social
  barrel no longer exports `useGsavFollow`, and the import-boundary verifier now
  allows catalog to compose `FollowButton` while rejecting direct catalog
  imports of `features/social/useGsavFollow.ts`.
- Refined social GSAV client ownership by adding
  `features/social/socialAdapter.ts` as the only social importer of
  `@opsiclear/gsav-client` and `services/supabase`. `savedScenesStore` and
  `useGsavFollow` now call adapter functions, `services/gsav.ts` no longer
  re-exports social helpers or social query types, and social query-shape tests
  live in `features/social/socialAdapter.test.ts`.
- Hardened `scripts/verify-release-readiness.js` so
  `docs/GSAV_NATIVE_QA.md` section `## Release Evidence Requirements` must stay
  aligned with the hard-gated release-readiness categories. The expected rows
  are derived from `requiredEvidence`, so route, negative, runtime, artifact,
  installed-APK, generated-version, range-probe, and dry-run categories cannot
  drift silently.
- Updated ADR 0002 validation checks to include import-boundary, docs-drift,
  and publish-readiness gates, then added docs-drift coverage so the ADR cannot
  regress to a weaker validation list.
- Hardened the sibling-feature allowlist metadata so the verifier now rejects
  entries that omit an owner or exit criteria, and updated the feature contract
  ledger with owner/removal details for the current allowlist entries.
- Strengthened the architecture-contract gate so the import-boundary verifier
  now prints owner and exit criteria for each sibling-feature allowlist entry,
  making exception ownership reviewable from the verifier output instead of
  only from prose docs.
- Expanded the external GSAV package policy scan to cover all `scripts/` files.
  `scripts/gsav-runtime-smoke-bridge.js` remains the only script-level
  `@opsiclear/gsav-bridge` importer, and focused tests now create a temporary
  rogue script to prove direct `@opsiclear/gsav-client` imports fail.
- Hardened the external GSAV package policy so unknown
  `@opsiclear/gsav-*` package names are rejected until the package, importers,
  rationale, and focused allow/reject tests are explicitly added to the policy.
- Strengthened the external GSAV package allowlist itself so every package rule
  must declare importer regexes, rationale, owner, and exit criteria before the
  import-boundary verifier accepts it.
- Enforced the sibling-feature allowlist budget directly in the import-boundary
  verifier. The gate now rejects growth beyond the current 14-entry budget
  unless the budget and focused tests are intentionally updated.
- Folded the latest three-agent implementation-plan review into the active plan.
  The plan now has a detailed execution sequence that separates repository
  verification from Android/iOS, production-host, APK/manifest, installed-APK,
  and GitHub Actions validation. It also records a release-candidate identity
  rule, a stricter exception policy, and follow-up architecture decisions for
  the `features/player/index.ts` helper barrel and saved-scene lookup ownership,
  both now resolved locally.
  Contributor-facing gates now include `npm ci`, `git diff --check`,
  `GSAV_RANGE_PROBE_URL`, `npm run verify:release-artifact`,
  `npm run android:installed-smoke`, and
  `npm run verify:release-evidence-bundle`, with docs-drift markers guarding
  those controls.
- Hardened release-readiness exceptions so a row that uses `exception` must
  include scoped fields: `gate`, `owner`, `reason`, `affected`, approver or a
  GitHub issue/run link, and unexpired `revisit`. The verifier rejects weak
  exception text and mismatched exception gates, while still treating candidate
  identity, production range-probe proof, APK/manifest checksums, and dry-run
  identity as non-bypassable publish evidence.

metadata enforcement, script-level GSAV package policy scanning, and unknown
GSAV package namespace rejection plus external package policy metadata
validation: typecheck, lint, import boundaries, dependency audit, docs drift,
full tests, coverage, and `git diff --check` pass. The focused
docs/import/readiness suite passes at 3 files / 77 tests, and the full suite

## Remaining Validation Gaps

- Android device/emulator WebView QA has not been run in this environment.
- iOS WKWebView QA has not been run in this environment.
- Android release-profile build, APK artifact scan, and installed-release APK
  smoke have not been run here:
  `adb`, `emulator`, and `java` are unavailable, and no generated `android/`
  directory is present.
- The release artifact verifier is unit-tested with synthetic bundle/manifest
  text and wired into CI, but has not yet been proven against a real Gradle APK
  or the actual merged manifest path on this machine. The exact produced APK
  also still needs `npm run android:installed-smoke` or equivalent
  install/launch smoke against the production GSAV host, with `apkSha256`
  and installed `versionCode` matched to `release-evidence/dry-run-summary.json`
  and the inspected CI artifact URL recorded.
- The release publish path has local candidate-freeze remediation but still
  needs production validation before it can be trusted for a real publish.
  Current tooling can verify non-publishing `workflow_dispatch` evidence and
  publish-intent artifact evidence through separate
  `verify:release-evidence-bundle` modes. The release workflow now consumes a
  fixed payload SHA through `candidate_ref`, refuses signoff diffs outside
  QA/audit/evidence files, avoids release-time version bumps, commits, and
  pushes, and `verify:release-readiness` compares completed rows to
  `RELEASE_CANDIDATE_SHA` while rejecting source/workflow/package changes after
  payload capture. This still needs a real production-secret dry run and final
  publish-readiness pass before release signoff.
- The production `.gsav` range probe is wired into release CI through
  `GSAV_RANGE_PROBE_URL`, and a non-publishing `workflow_dispatch` dry run is
  available, but no real production-secret workflow run has proven it yet.
- The release workflow now runs production GSAV host preflight, but that CI path
  still needs a run with real production secrets before it can be counted as
  validated.
- Release-candidate identity ordering is now locally verified in the workflow
  and production-config verifier, including `candidate_ref`, payload/signoff
  diff inspection, `release-candidate.txt`, `RELEASE_CANDIDATE_SHA`, no
  release-time version bump/commit/push, production preflight/runtime smoke
  after identity capture, and `gh release create --target`. The
  release-evidence-bundle verifier also rejects preflight/runtime-smoke JSON
  whose `diveoCommit` does not match `releaseCandidateSha`. This still needs a
  real production-secret dry run before the release evidence can be counted as
  validated.
- The release evidence bundle verifier is unit-tested and wired into the
  release workflow, but it still needs a real production-secret workflow run to
  validate an actual uploaded `release-evidence/**` artifact, APK, and merged
  manifest.
- The dry-run bundle now requires `release-evidence/no-publish-side-effect.txt`
  with post-run git status, HEAD, remote ref, `gh release view`, and
  `githubReleasePresent=false`; this is locally verified with synthetic
  evidence, but still needs a real production-secret dry run to prove against
  the live repository and release tag.
- Production GSAV host identity is still a validation dependency. Preflight and
  runtime smoke record concrete host/build identity, and production preflight
  must fetch `GSAV_HOST_IDENTITY_URL` with `hostIdentityVerified=true`,
  `hostIdentity.url=<metadata URL>`,
  `hostIdentity.expectedIdentity=<GSAV_HOSTING_COMMIT>`, and
  `hostIdentity.observedIdentity` matching `GSAV_HOSTING_COMMIT`; an env-only
  value is not enough for a publish decision unless the audit records
  no-publish.
- `npm run verify:release-readiness` now treats the durable local JS
  runtime-smoke row as pre-release evidence only. Publish paths remain blocked
  until the QA evidence table replaces the remaining 29 publish evidence rows
  pending, including Release validation prerequisites, production runtime-smoke,
  durable device, negative, range-probe, artifact, installed-release APK smoke,
  generated version metadata, and dry-run evidence.
- Completed-row hardening is now implemented in `verify:release-readiness`; the
  remaining release-readiness gap is collecting durable device, artifact,
  generated version metadata, range-probe, and dry-run evidence that satisfies
  those stricter checks.
- Local JS runtime smoke currently covers Explore, diagnostics, and watch route
  readiness/playback/error, but production JS runtime-smoke evidence remains
  pending because publish-readiness now rejects local/private GSAV URLs and
  unavailable host identity. Native progress clear behavior is unit-covered
  through `progressBridge`, `gsavProgressStore`, and native WebView
  bridge-message routing tests, including trusted `GSAV_ENDED` clearing resume
  state. Android/iOS WebView-observed `GSAV_ENDED` evidence remains pending.
- Auth-init session gating now has helper-level and host-side-effect coverage,
  including source-level guards that native WebView and Expo web iframe callers
  use the auth-state-aware helpers. Device evidence is still required for the
  observed Android/iOS bridge message timeline. Runtime smoke now also requires
  compatible `GSAV_BRIDGE_READY` version/minVersion metadata on the hosted routes.
- `GSAV_FIRST_FRAME` is still observational rather than required; the latest
  Windows Chromium smoke did not emit it.
- Follow controls now compose through social-owned
  `features/social/FollowButton.tsx`; catalog no longer imports
  `features/social/useGsavFollow.ts` directly.
- Remaining structure refinement is limited to catalog/social/player
  composition contracts: catalog must keep passing IDs, display values, placement
  props, and callbacks into `SaveSceneButton`, `FollowButton`, and
  `ContinueWatchingPill` without reading social stores, resume stores, bridge
  state, Supabase clients, or follow reconciliation rules.
- Android version metadata is verified at the source-config level and exposed as

- Unused direct-dependency cleanup is complete for the known non-Bilibili
  package set. Remaining dependency audit risk is accepted only for the known
  10 moderate Expo/Sentry tooling-chain advisories through `2026-07-31`; the
  unresolved native-build validation is proving Android/iOS native builds still

## Negative Fixture Ownership

The QA runbook requires an exact trigger before a negative row can become
release evidence. All current negative validation cases now have named routes,
flags, commands, or controls; the remaining work is capturing distinct Android
and iOS evidence. If a future trigger is removed or a new negative case is added
without a runnable path, record it in the QA backlog and this ownership table
as owner-blocked until a route, flag, command, or hosted control is named.
Same-origin product-path blocking is a route-packet subcheck, not a separate
negative readiness row: use `/gsav-diagnostics` with
`EXPO_PUBLIC_GSAV_QA_CONTROLS=1`, tap `Same-origin`, and attach the
`/creator/qa-native-blocked` `Navigation blocked` result to
`sameOriginProductPathOutcome`.

| Negative case | Current trigger status | Owner | Next action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Missing host config | Available through app config by launching an embedded route without `EXPO_PUBLIC_GSAV_WEB_URL` | native release owner | Capture Android and iOS evidence showing native config/error UI instead of a blank WebView |
| Host offline/retry | Available by stopping or blocking the GSAV preview/staging host while `/watch/test` is open, then retrying after recovery | native release owner | Capture Android and iOS evidence showing retry UI and recovery |
| Cross-origin navigation | Available through `/gsav-diagnostics` when built with `EXPO_PUBLIC_GSAV_QA_CONTROLS=1`; tap `Cross-origin` | native release owner | Capture Android and iOS evidence showing the QA panel reports blocked external navigation and the app stays on the trusted route |
| Unsupported renderer | Available through `/gsav-diagnostics` when built with `EXPO_PUBLIC_GSAV_QA_CONTROLS=1`; tap `Unsupported` | native release owner | Capture Android and iOS evidence showing the native overlay reports the QA unsupported renderer state without a blank WebView |
| Auth initialization gate | Available through `EXPO_PUBLIC_GSAV_QA_AUTH_DELAY_MS=5000`; open `/gsav-diagnostics` or `/watch/test` while auth restore is delayed | native release owner | Capture Android and iOS evidence showing no clear-session bridge message before auth initialization |
| Ended playback | Available through `/gsav-diagnostics` when built with `EXPO_PUBLIC_GSAV_QA_CONTROLS=1`; tap `Ended`, then reopen `/watch/test` | native release owner | Capture Android and iOS evidence showing saved progress for `test` is cleared |
